               2. INTRODUCTION TO SOUTH PARS GAS FIELD


           South Pars Gas-Condensate Field, and its southern extension, the North Dome, are

             located in the Persian Gulf, which is separated by the international marine border. This

              field is the most giant known gas field in the world which is along the huge NNE-SSW

             trending Qatar-Fars Arch. South Pars Field is actually the northern part of the field,

             located in Iranian waters the nearest distance of the field from the Iranian shore-line is

            approximately 100 Km. Water depth is about 60-70 meters in Iranian water. Dimension

             of the South Pars - North Dome Field is over 13,000 square kilometers and the vertical

             closure on top Kangan horizon exceeds 600 meters. (Figure 1).

           South Pars Field was discovered in 1990 by drilling well SP-01 that encountered gas

              reservoir. In 1991-1992 the field was confirmed by drilling three additional wells SP-

             02, SP-03 and SP-04. Further nineteen appraisal / delineation or appraisal / production

             wells were  drilled  in 1998-2010 on the  structure. Also more than one hundred

           development wells have been drilled in several development areas or phases of South

            Pars area so far which are in production now.

          Kangan Formation and Upper Dalan Member of Dehram Group collectively host South

            Pars Gas Reservoir. These carbonate formations were deposited in a shallow marine

            environment during a general regional marine transgression that began in the middle of

           Permian and lasted until the Early Triassic period. Within these reservoirs (Kangan and

           Dalan  Formations  which  are  equivalent  to  the  Khuff  Formation  in  Arabian

            nomenclature), there are four main producing reservoir zones, which from bottom to

            top are K4, K3, K2 and K1 and are separated by non-depositional surfaces or

             equivalent boundaries. In general typical matrix porosity ranges from 8% to 20%. The

             reservoir is over pressured with an estimated pressure gradient of 1.40kg/cm2/10m -

           1.45kg/cm2/10m at top of the uppermost structural high of the Kangan Formation.

           Depending on the different levels, the Condensate Gas Ratio differs as the K1 level

             contains the leaner gas while the K2 should feature the richest condensate content and

             the K4 level features both intermediate gas and condensate content. An extension of a

           massive anhydrite layer, known as Nar member, separates K4 from the underlying K5

          member that reveals poor reservoir characteristics in SP-01 and SPD12B-08. On the

             basis  of  the  available  data  the  quality of  the  reservoir appear  to be  vertically

             dramatically variable, due to several events occurred during the deposition as well as

            during the digenesis phases. It is considered that Dashtak Formation is known as a good

              seal for these reservoir layers and this petroleum system is completed by the nearly

             thick shally formation which is called Sarchahan and it seems that acts as an active

            source rock.


             2.1.       Location Data of Phases in South Pars Gas Field

            Location map of Phases 17B & 18B and respective adjacent phases as well as location

             of appraisal wells is shown in Figure 2 and 3. Phases 17 and 18 of the South Pars Gas

             Field is going to be developed by drilling four vertical wells (2 appraisal wells and 2

             learning wells) and also forty deviated wells from four platforms (SPD17A, SPD17B,

         SPD18A and SPD18B). The location of the platforms and general information of the

             desired wells have been depicted in the Figure 2-3

            6.   HEALTH, SAFETY AND ENVIRONMENT


                     6.1.  HEALTH & SAFETY

          The overall strategic objectives of HSE management during drilling, completion, clean

           up and testing operations are:

             To ensure the health and safety of personnel.

             To safeguard the integrity of wells, facilities and installations.

             Prevent adverse effects on the environment and third parties.

             Zero Rating

                    -   Spill to the sea

                    -  Dropped objects

                    -  Well control occurrence

             H2S, Diverter and Well Control Certification and drill procedures

             Daily safety meetings and weekly fire and emergency drills

             Housekeeping daily

             HSE will focus on:

             On/offloading materials to prevent droppage of materials and equipment

             Communication to all personnel. Enforce communication to create involvement for

                    all personnel based on acting as a ‘Team’. Radio communication for entire drilling

                 unit and headphone for specific work in areas of high background noise.

             Pre-job safety meetings  start of each crew change and recorded IADC report.

                Additional safety meeting for every key event occurring i.e. Casing, cementing, and

          BOP setup and pressure test.





This Document Is The Sole Property Of D.D.C. All Information Contained Herein May Neither Be Reproduced,Used,
Disclosed, Transmitted Or In Any Way Made Public Without The Written Permission Of The D.D.C.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 24 از 181 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                 Drilling Program of Vertical Well(Learning Well)
                            SPD18B-08



                                            Doc. No.: DDC-12-14-RE-005-01
     REV.: 01
DATE: 26 FEB. 2013                                                       Page 24 of 169
                                            Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

             All crews trained H2S. Regular H2S drills will be held.

             All crews trained diverter drills. Drills to be held each crew daily while drilling

               conductor and surface casing hole sections.

             Derrick will be inspected continuous basis to notify loose objects and equipment.

             All crews should report any non-conformances and noted on IADC reports.

             As part of the contractor selection process, evaluate the contractor's management of

           HSE during well construction activities and ensure that the HSE-MS is suitable and

                   sufficient.

          The HSE-MS system is the management framework for managing HSE, and covers

          management of HSE during well construction activities. The well construction HSE

            case is a component of the HSEMS.

          The drilling and other service contractors together with the Contractor will produce

            bridging documents to assure that the interfaces between the Contractor and the service

              contactors' HSE-MS are well understood and managed.


                     6.2.  HEALTH AND SAFETY HAZARDS

            Current major hazards include: exposure to H2S, exposure to heat radiation, exposure

              to  chemicals, heavy  lift  operations, boat  collisions with  Jacket  / Rig  ; marine

           movement, blowouts, transport risks, slips, trips and falls. A full hazard register in the

        HSE case will address these and will lead to controls such as journey management,

            permit to work, contingency, emergency plan and etc. There will also be a Manual of

            Permitted Operations.

                 The following safety meetings/drills to be held:



                                                 Frequency
                  Safety Meeting/Drills

                       Rig Induction                  Immediately on arrival to rig

                   Pre-Tour Safety Meetings           At the start of each 12hr Tour

                      Pre-Job Toolbox talks                Prior to specific rig operations

                Daily Service Company Meeting                 Daily

                 Weekly Safety Meetings                 Weekly

           H2S /Fire /Abandon /Man Over Drills            Weekly

                  Well Control/BOP Drills                 Weekly



          The STOP System has been implemented and a weekly target of 50 STOP cards per

          week has been set to ensure that Safety on the rig continues to be a high priority for the

             duration of the well.

            Following an overall assessment of the environmental effects resulting -from the

               activities involved in the South Pars drilling program, three key issues were identified

             as being potentially environmentally significant and requiring further study. These

            were:

              1.  Discharge to sea of water based mud and cuttings: All drilling for the South Pars

                wells will be carried out    using water based mud. As such as all used mud and

                 cuttings will be discharged to sea as a means of disposal. Disposed cuttings would

              be expected to either sink into or become incorporated into the seabed soils, or to be

                dispersed widely  into  the water column and  surrounding seabed  with  little

                 detectable effect on biological communities.





This Document Is The Sole Property Of D.D.C. All Information Contained Herein May Neither Be Reproduced,Used,
Disclosed, Transmitted Or In Any Way Made Public Without The Written Permission Of The D.D.C.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 26 از 181 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                 Drilling Program of Vertical Well(Learning Well)
                            SPD18B-08



                                            Doc. No.: DDC-12-14-RE-005-01
     REV.: 01
DATE: 26 FEB. 2013                                                       Page 26 of 169
                                            Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

              2.  Emissions to air from power generation and well testing: The planned combustion

              from power generation  is industry average with an expected industry average

               environmental effect. The subsequent effect on local air quality issues will be

                 undetectable. The planned  well  testing combustion  is high  for  the  activity.

              However, the technical justification for the extent of the well testing centers upon

                 the requirement to ensure that the wells can achieve their target production rates and

                 to establish accurate condensate to gas ratios. Considering the assessed significance

                of the environmental impact of the well test emissions in relation to the technical

                  justification for the program, no mitigation measures are mandated for the program.

              3.  Accidental liquid hydrocarbon spills: The most likely cause of a spill in relation to

               South Pars drilling is of a small diesel spill during bunkering. Given that the

                prospect is gas/condensate there is no likelihood of a   large crude spill from the

                wells themselves. A very low  possibility of a large crude  spill would be a

                  collision between the rig and a crude oil carrier. As the Persian Gulf is one of the

              most important oil "transport commercial waterways in the world this source of

                 spillage remains a possibility. Fuel should be transferred to the rig during the hours

                of daylight unless it is an emergency.


                     6.3.  SHALLOW GAS

         No shallow gas has been detected in any offset wells. There has also been no evidence

             of shallow gas within the deeper section neither beneath present locations in area

            surveyed.


                     6.4.  EXPECTED H2S AND CO2

         H2S is present in the formation fluids from the Jahrum-Ilam formations, approximately

              starting from ±600m to the setting of the 18-5/8” casing  at about ±972m (Ilam

            Formation). The H2S shows up at surface as a consequence of the circulation losses in

             the form of sulphurous water. The H2S content measured during these shows on the




This Document Is The Sole Property Of D.D.C. All Information Contained Herein May Neither Be Reproduced,Used,
Disclosed, Transmitted Or In Any Way Made Public Without The Written Permission Of The D.D.C.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 27 از 181 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                 Drilling Program of Vertical Well(Learning Well)
                            SPD18B-08



                                            Doc. No.: DDC-12-14-RE-005-01
     REV.: 01
DATE: 26 FEB. 2013                                                       Page 27 of 169
                                            Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

             reference SP-8 well and SP-7 was high enough to saturate the sensors (i.e. in excess of

           100 ppm). Also the reservoir fluid contains a quite high content of H2S which has been

            confirmed from the results of the DST tests and PVT analysis. The H2S content

           measured are in the range of 5000-10000 ppm; the CO2 in the range of 20000 ppm.

          The  drilling crew must be trained  in the use of breathing apparatus  sets. H2S

           emergency  drills must be conducted  frequently,  starting from  the beginning  of

             operations. Adequate sensors and alarms must be installed for the scope of monitoring

             the H2S releases. The mud properties must be constantly monitored for H2S and CO2

               effects. The appropriate quantity of H2S scavengers shall be available to be mixed with

         mud if required by contingent situations.

            Procedures set-forth in the HSE document shall be enforced at all times during the

              drilling of the wells.


                     6.5.  WELL INTERFERENCE

                  It is imperative that the conductor casing not exceed 0.5 degrees to eliminate any

              collision of adjacent well bores. Surveys should be taken every half single drilled to

            ensure that this inclination does not exceed at any point along well bore path. Casing

             points for each well will vary to limit future well interference.

          The surface casing kickoff points will be +/- 210 m RT TVD to allow as much

         minimum variance from vertical for installation of the SSSV and hydraulic lines. Spider

           and well collision plots will be run and updated to limit potential of interference.




            7.   LEARNING WELL OBJECTIVE (SPD18B-08)

          The objectives for the learning well is to drill the well vertically into the reservoir target

             area as programmed to achieve production of 100 mm scf/d. The ultimate goals are to

                drill and complete each well safe and within budgeted time and cost.

           10.   GEOLOGICAL FORECAST


                    10.1.  REGIONAL GEOLOGY

        The South Pars Gas- Condensate Field and its southern extension, the North Dome, are

          located in the interior platform of the Arabian Shelf structural province. The field is a part

          of huge NNE-SSW trending Qatar- Fars Arch that is one of the major structural elements

           in central Persian Gulf area. It is also relatively close to the Zagros Mobile Belt Mountain

          forelands located northwards. So the geological history of the South Pars was as effected

        by events occurring in the quite stable Arabian Plate as by those in the surrounding more

          active area.

          Several reservoirs (oil and gas) were identified by the exploration and appraisal wells. The

            oil reservoirs of the South Pars are in Khami and Bangestan Group formations. The Gas-

         Condensate bearing reservoirs of this field belong to the Kangan and Dalan formations of

           Triassic and Permian age respectively. These carbonate formations were deposited in a

         shallow marine environment during a general marine transgression that began in the

         middle of Permian and lasted until the early Triassic. The basal section of Dashtak

          formation, which was formed throughout the lower, middle and upper parts of The

           Triassic period, is respected by argillaceous siltstone, shale and dolomite with anhydrite

           inter beds and the remaining middle-upper parts mainly consist of dolomite intercalation.

         Dashtak formation is known as good seal for Kangan-Dalan reservoirs .Within these

           reservoirs (Kangan and Dalan which are equivalent to the Khuff Formation in Arabian

          nomenclature), there are four main producing levels, which from top to bottom are K1,

        K2, K3 and K4 which are separated by anhydritic layers. Each of these levels has been

          divided to several subzones (Totally 16 subzones – TEC 2003; 12 subzones – total 1999).

      A massive anhydrite body, known as Nar member, separates the K4 from the underlying

       K5 member that exhibits poor reservoir characteristics.

                    10.2. GEOLOGICAL SETTING OF SOUTH PARS FIELD

        The South Pars Gas Field reservoir is shared with Qatar in median line and the portion,

           falling within Iranian water, was detected through the seismic survey shot in 1989 and

         1992. Since then, twenty exploration and appraisal  / delineation wells (SP-1 to SP-15,

        SPD10-08, SPD11-08, SPD2-04, SPD4-01and SPD12-C-8) have been drilled. The top of

           reservoir roughly sets around 2700m from the sea level and the wet gas (gas and

          condensate) is in a gross rock thickness of about 400M immediately below the reservoir

           top.

       K1 mainly consisted of dolomite, Packstone-Wackstone limestone and streaks of shale.

         There are also three rather thin anhydrite sub layers at the middle and in the lower half of

          the  layer. K2 mainly consisted of Wackstone, Packstone and Grainstone limestone

          interbedded with thick dolomite layers.

       K3 mostly consisted of Mudstone, Grainstone limestone interbedded with thick dolomite

          layers significant anhydrite interval occurs at the bottom part of K3.

       K4 mostly consisted of Packstone, Olitic Grainstone limestone, dolomite and rare thin

          layers of anhydrite.

       K1 thickness is about 112M. The rock quality is poor and its average porosity and water

           saturation is about 12 and 18 percent. K2 thickness is about 45M and its average porosity

         and water saturation is about 11 and 7 percent. K3 thickness is about 119M and its average

          porosity and water saturation is about 10 and 8 percent. K4 thickness is about 178M and

             its average porosity and water saturation is about 16 and 7 percent respectively.

        The difference in GWC at different well locations indicates a tilted gas-water contact.

         Based on the available information, there is a tilt in GWC from west south- west to east-

           north-east.

                    10.3. SOUTH PARS STRATIGRAPHY

       Among the exploration / delineation and development wells drilled in this area to date,

         only in one well, SP-01, a short penetration of ± 19 meters was made into the Faraghun

         Formation. All the other wells drilled in the South Pars have been terminated in the upper

           parts of the Nar Member, as the objective of the drilling was gas production, reservoir

           definition and / or fluid contact identification in the Kangan-Upper Dalan reservoirs. This

         provided reasonable stratigraphic knowledge down to the top of the Nar Member of the

         Dalan Formation. But, stratigraphic information of the deeper horizons has to come from

          the nearby areas of the sedimentary basin.


                    10.4. LITHO-STRATIGRAPHY


        The next table shows the stratigraphic position of the Kangan-Dalan reservoir in the South

          Pars Field and the Persian Gulf area. Reservoir is divided into five reservoir layers, K1 to

        K5. K1 plus K2 is equivalent to the Kangan and K3 plus K4 is equivalent to the Upper

         Dalan. K5 is assumed non-reservoir in the South Pars and put out of the field. The

           reservoir layers are separated by impermeable barriers. Therefore, the field consists of four

         independent reservoir layers. In this study, the four reservoir layers are named K1, K2,

        K3, and K4 according to their Iranian nomenclature, Kangan (KG) and Upper Dalan

         (UD). The only direct information available from the sediments underneath the Dalan

         Formation is limited to well SP-01. This well penetrates Nar Member, Lower Dalan, and

         about 19M of the Faraghun clastics. The Nar Member is about 174M thick in SP-01. It is

        composed of tight anhydrite and dolomite layers, fine-grained and argillaceous, anhydritic

           to pseudo-Olitic rocks, associated with argillaceous micritic limestone. The average

          thickness of the Upper Dalan Member is about 285M in the South Pars.

              It is composed of two major reservoir layers, UD-3 and UD-4 (K3 & K4): B The average

          thickness of UD-4 (K4) in the South Pars is about 165M. It is composed of six litho-units

         which from bottom to top are as follows: anhydritic dolomite unit (UD_4-6) grading

         According to the bio-stratigraphic and stable isotope studies performed to date, the Dalan

          of a stromatolite /thrombolite unit over a breccia interval.

        Review of all available core descriptions indicates that lateral variations of litho-facies are

         minor over the field. This is well shown and confirmed by the correlation of electro-facies

          (rock types) and petrophysical multi-mineral Evaluations for the vertical wells of the field.

          Anhydrite: milky white to white, soft, washable, chalky, amorphous, crystalline in parts.

              b. K1
         Dolomite: mudstone  to  Packstone,  beige,  moderately  hard  to  hard,  crystalline  to

           cryptocrystalline, occasionally with pelloids  , pebbles and shell fragment  , locally mud

          cracks in mudstone  , slightly argillaceous in parts  , with thin layers of anhydrite ,very

        good to fair vuggy and intercrystalline Porosity.

         Limestone : mudstone , Wackstone , off white to white , cream to light brown , brownish

         gray , soft ,amorphous , chalky , cryptocrystalline to microcrystalline , brittle to crumbly ,

         washable , slightly argillaceous , traces re-crystallized calcite , poor to no visible porosity.

          Anhydrite: white, off white, soft to firm, amorphous, cryptocrystalline, washable.

                 c. K2
          Limestone: mudstone, Wackstone, Grainstone, white to off white, cream, light gray, light

         brown, soft to firm , microcrystalline to fine crystalline , dolomitic and anhydritic in parts

           slightly argillaceous .

         Dolomite: alternation of Grainstone with Olites and cream Packstone with moldic and

        vuggy fair to good porosity, moderately hard to hard.

       15. Dalan Formation
               a. K3
         Dolomite: gray to dark gray, dark brown, brittle, fine crystalline, slightly argillaceous,

         hard to moderately hard, locally calcareous, micro-sucrosic, traces Olites and pelloids,

         poor to visible porosity .

          Anhydrite: milky white, soft to firm, amorphous, microcrystalline to cryptocrystalline,

         washable.

         Limestone: mudstone  , Wackstone  , light gray , off white  , light grayish brown  , soft  ,

        amorphous  ,cryptocrystalline  to  microcrystalline   ,  chalky  in  parts   ,  moderately

         moderately hard to hard, compact, cryptocrystalline to microcrystalline, sucrosic, no

          visual porosity.


                    10.7. SAMPLING

       Mud loggers under the direction of the well site geologist will be responsible for catching

         samples according to the following program.

        The cutting samples are required to be collected in 2-4 meters intervals to identify the

          formation tops and reservoir lithology types. The above sampling interval may be altered

          as required by the well site geologist.

        Where drilling is slower or there is a marker of geological importance, hydrocarbon shows

          or indication of hole problems, the sample interval will be advised by well site geologist.

                    10.8. WASHED / UNWASHED CUTTINGS

       Two sets of washed and dried cuttings to be prepared in paper envelopes. Two sets of

        unwashed bulk cuttings samples are to be collected in cloth bags. The samples should be

            air dried bagged and packed in steel/wood boxes. Sample distribution and amounts will be

          advised by well site geologist. All sample bags are to be clearly marked with well number,

         depth and set number.

           14.   RESERVOIR CHARACTERISTICS





           15.  CASING POINTS AND DEPTH

                 26" conductor pipe is set and cemented at approximately ±100 m below sea bed.

                 18 5/8” casing shoe is planned 10 m TVD into the Ilam Formation. The prognosis

                     for casing depth is ±982 m from RTE in Ilam formation.

                 13 3/8” casing shoe is planned 10 m TVD into the Hith Formation. The Hith

                   formation is an Anhydrite, and picked by sample. The prognosis for casing depth is

                ±1492 m.

                 9 5/8” casing shoe is to be set after the last shale layer (about 10 m TVD) into the

                 Upper Kangan formation. The Upper Kangan Formation has a very constant

                    thickness (25-30 m) and the thin shale streaks are distinctive and picked by sample.

                The prognosis for casing depth is ± 2659m, 9 5/8” casing will be crossed over to 10-

                   3/4” casing at +/- 180 m to accommodate the sub surface safety valve.

                 7” Liner shoe will be set at TD (±3153 m MD) or base of K4. The prognosis of liner

                  hanger point is ± 2539 m MD(120 m O.Lap is considered), 7” liner L-80 covers K1,

                 K2, K3 and K4 levels.7” Tubing and Liner should be considered as contingency if

                   hole problems dictate to commit an extra string of casing.


                    15.1. TOTAL DEPTH (TD)

                    Total depth (TD) will be 30 m MD after target point and target point is the low

                    perforation point which is 30 m minimum above the estimated GWC. TD will Be

                 3153 m MD RTE / 3153 m TVD RTE.

           17.  MUD LOGGING CONSIDERATION

                The mud logging services for all wells shall start from the beginning of drilling 24”

                   hole and will be continued to the total depth (TD) of the well. The services shall

                    include the collection of cuttings, and samples of cuttings, lithologic description,

                    calcimetry real time gas analysis with improved degassing system and computer

                   software to calculate the concentration of hydrocarbons C1-C5, and gases like H2S

                 and CO2  It is recommended to cut thin section (from both core and cutting) from

                   top hole to the TD. All the drilling parameters shall be presented in two display and

                  composite and drilling graphical panels. The details of these panels will be decided

                 on with the mud logging Service Company before the start of the job.

                The well site geologist will supervise the operation of the mud logging unit from

                  16" hole section and ensure that samples are caught and labeled satisfactorily, and

                      that a thorough and accurate formation evaluation log is produced at 1:500 scale and

                   a pressure evaluation log at 1:1200 scale on continuous basis. Final films of these

                    logs are to be supplied upon completion of geological operation on the well. The

                   following responsibilities should be carried out by mud logging service personnel.

                          1. To inform immediately drilling representative and well site geologist in case

                           of any show or unusual drilling conditions.

                          2.  If drilling problem occur or are anticipated that might be detrimental to

                      optimum mud logging quality, notify representative or well site geologist

                          immediately, unless the problem is minor and can be corrected personally.

                          3.  Regularly check fluorescence of rig oil, pipe dope and mud additives.

                          4. A sample of each mud additive in the well is to be saved when diesel or

                           other oil-based drilling fluid are added to mud system, sample is to be run

                         through chromatograph for reference.

                          5. Any condition detrimental to safe or efficient operation is to be reported

                        promptly to representative and well site geologist, these include but not


This Document Is The Sole Property Of D.D.C. All Information Contained Herein May Neither Be Reproduced,Used,
Disclosed, Transmitted Or In Any Way Made Public Without The Written Permission Of The D.D.C.

                            limited  to,  increasing gas  readings,  increasing  trip  or connection  gas,

                         changing mud  salinity or unusually large amounts of sloughing and/or

                           caving.

                          6.  All equipments should be checked and calibrated on a daily basis, including

                                total gas recorder, gas chromatograph and H2S sensors.

                          7. Lag time should be checked daily by pumping a known substance.

              18.  BOP TEST POLICY

                     18.1.1   PRESSURE TEST FREQUENCY BOP-STACK

                 After installing stack on well head.
                 Any time a new casing string is run and cemented.
                 Once every 7 Days or 14 days or Max 21 days.
                  Prior to run a DST or production test assembly.
                 Any time required by Company.
                 Any time BOP opens for repairing or replacing RAMS or Bonnet Seal.


                     18.1.2   FUNCTION TESTS

                 The pipe rams and BOP valves should be operated at least once every shift.
                 Blind/shear rams shall be operated every round trip in the hole.
                 The annular preventer shall be operated when the scheduled routine BOP tests are
                   performed.


                     18.1.3   DURATION OF TESTS

                 The BOP 300psi low-pressure tests will be performed first. They are to be held for a
                min period of 5min.
                 High-pressure tests are held for a minimum of 10mins. The maximum acceptable
                    pressure drop over this 10mins period is 100psi.

              19.     24” HOLE SECTION (SURFACE CASING)


                   19.1.   DRILLING OBJECTIVE

            Primary objective is to shutoff/seal the highly fractured dolomitic Jahrum Formation. The

            planned setting depth for the 18-5/8” casing will be +/- 10 m TVD into the Ilam

             Formation.


                   19.2.   DRILLING EVENT SUMMARY

                    a.  Skid and position rig over slot 08

                  b.  Nipple up 30” -500 psi annular diverter and bell nipple

                    c. Make up 24” BHA and stand up enough d.p’s to 24” section TD and RIH

                  d.  Perform H2S and diverter drills

                    e.  Drill 26” shoe track, clean rat hole using seawater with hi‐vis sweeps, take

                  samples for geological requirements

                       f.  Drill to top of Jahrum Formation

                  g.  Circulate hole clean

                  h.  Meanwhile perform H2S drill before drilling into the Jahrum Formation

                         i.   Drill 24” hole to TD at 908m RT TVD include 3 m rat hole (10 m into the Ilam

                  Formation)

                         j.  Perform wiper trip one single into asmari Formation and back to TD

                  k.  Circulate hole clean and then spot 1.2 SG hi‐vis mud to top of Jahrum Fm, POOH

                         l.  Run and cement 18‐5/8” casing with the 20‐3/4” Casing Head Housing.

             m. Fill every joint with SW to avoid collapse of casing. Ensure proper CHH

                      orientation.

                   19.3.   DRILLING HAZARDS

       Water Influx / H2S

        Sulphurous water influx has occurred from the Jahrum Formation.

       Stuck Pipe, BHA Packed Off, Excessive Reaming

        Both the Jahrum and Sachun Formations drill strings have become stuck due to

          Partial and/or total loss circulation, packoff and poor hole cleaning (tight hole).

     A majority of offset wells required the casing to be washed and reamed to bottom

        due to insufficient hole cleaning and build up of solids due in inability to clean

         hole as a result of total loss circulation.

       Loss Circulation

          Partial to total loss circulation occurs in the highly fractured dolomitic Jahrum

         Formation. Partial to total loss circulation has also occurred in the Sachun

         Formation. Asmari Formation partial loss of circulation has been observed.

       Effect on Drilling Parameters

       The Jahrum Formation causes mechanical sticking and irregular rotary torque

        which can cause serious damage and fatigue to all down hole equipment. This has

        caused drill pipe twist offs.

This Document Is The Sole Property Of D.D.C. All Information Contained Herein May Neither Be Reproduced,Used,
Disclosed, Transmitted Or In Any Way Made Public Without The Written Permission Of The D.D.C.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 58 از 181 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                   Drilling Program of Vertical Well(Learning Well)
                             SPD18B-08



                                              Doc. No.: DDC-12-14-RE-005-01
       REV.: 01
  DATE: 26 FEB. 2013                                                       Page 58 of 169
                                              Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

        Both Jahrum and Sachun Formations are very abrasive which causes excessive and

         rapid wear on stabilizers, bit gauge wear (under gauge hole) and loss of hole angle.

        Using insert drill bits will help to reduce the effect of the abrasiveness of these

         formations and in addition to the insert bits, the use of roller reamers, shock subs

        and constant inspection of equipment all will help alleviate the problem that occurs.

       Bit Balling

        Because of the loss circulation and influence of both gas and fluid entrainment maintaining

         rheological properties of the drilling fluids is virtually impossible. To help over compensate for

          this to occur it is recommended to ensure that all bits used should be bits with center jets. The

         additional flow area along with high pump rate eliminates bit balling from occurring.

       Collapse Casing

        Another effect as a result of the circulation loss and inability to achieve efficient

         hole cleaning there were two cases where during cement displacement exceeded

         the collapse pressure of the casing causing them to collapse and a need to cut and

          sidetrack. During cementing and cement displacing operations the pressure

        should not exceed 80% of the collapse rating of the 18‐5/8” casing (est. 700 psi.).

         Collapse of casing can also occur when inner string cement operations are carried out.


                   19.4.   DRILLING PIT FALLS

             Check and calibrate all pits, gas and flow sensors. Function tests the alarms.

             Limit the circulation rate while drilling below the C.P to avoid hole wash out.

             Keep the mud density as low as practicable accounting for the losses that will be

               experienced during the drilling through the Jahrum & Sachun formations.

              If dictated by the need of ensuring good hole cleaning, limit the drilling rate accordingly.

                 Bit balling and BHA pack off are possible while drilling down to +/- 600 m.

              Circulation losses are expected from +/- 600 m to TD. Be aware that drilling through the

                highly fractured Jahrum often leads to mechanical stuck pipe problems.

             Bringing up the mud pumps to minimize hydraulic shock on the formations. Ensure that

              enough amount and types of LCM are available.

              If Complete loss happened in jahrum formation Fill Up Hole with SW from Annulus to

              have Floating Mud Cap in Hole.

             At casing point, perform a short trip to C.P shoe. On bottom check tight hole and/or

                hole fill.

             Perform float valve function test after shoe & Collar joint.

             Intermediate circulation may be performed if required by hole condition.

             Moderate the casing running speed to minimize surge and reduce risk of inducing

                 circulation losses.

             Cement casing according to cementing program. Slurry recipes will be lab tested in due

               time prior the Cementing job.

              It is recommended 20-3/4” CHH to be hand make Up on 1 nipple prior to start running

          CSG on the main deck to in order to save rig time.(2 eye lift’s should be available on rig

                   site for easy handling of 20-3/4” CHH + 2 short V.R plug).

                               to 1 single placed in the mouse hole (connection in mouse hole).

                    19.9. WELL PROFILE


               •  Casing shoe will be set +/- 10 m TVD into Ilam Formation.

               •  The plan for 24” hole section is to drill the well vertically from surface to total
                    depth.
               •  The plan is to drill the section with two bit runs.

               •  The performance BHA for the first BHA Run, comprised of an 11-1/4” PDM
               motor and jar/accelerator assembly for performance drilling of Top section from 190
                  to +/- 600 m , top of jahrum formation.(to be confirmed)

               •  The BHA No. 1 will be POOH prior to drilling into the Jahrum Formation at +/-
              600 m.

               • BHA No. 2 will be composed of a rotary hold/build assembly, 23-1/2” String
                       Stabilizer, and jar/accelerator to drill to TD.

               •  All BHA components and DP to be drifted.

               •  Never run Jar/Accelerator in neutral point.


                   19.10. SURVEYING

                 The section will be surveyed using TOTCO tool every 300 m intervals down to
                    top of Jahrum. Depends on hole condition, TOTCO could be dropped at TD, prior
                      to POOH.


                   19.11.   DRILLING FLUIDS PROGRAM

             The 24-inch interval will be drilled with sea water and hi-viscous mud sweeps.

            As this is a large diameter hole and the hole cuttings removal mechanism will be a

               combination of high annular velocities and hi-viscous sweeps. The viscous sweeps are

             composed of Guar Gum and Pre-hydrated Gel (PHG).This section will be drilled

               through the ASMARI, JAHRUM and ILAM formations. Each of these, have their own

                  particular problems to be dealt with and the engineers are to be aware of these prior to

                  drilling these. From offset well analysis it is proven that the major problem is the down-

                hole losses and its consequent loss of hydrostatic. This loss of hydrostatic will result in

                bore-hole failure, poor hole cleaning, pack off and stuck pipe.

                In order to minimize the chances on the above problems, the hole is to be cleaned.

            Due to logistical constraints, it is possible that drill water might be in short supply. The

                engineers however are to ensure that sufficient drill water is kept on location in order to

                prepare pre-hydrated gel (PHG) for at least the final displacement. A large volume of

                  fluid will have to be turned over in this section and emphasis will be placed on pit

             management and drill water logistics. The engineer should endeavor to have all the pits

                   full with gel based hi-viscous mud in order to avoid ending up in a “catching up”

                  situation.

                All hole-cleaning pills are to be formulated with H2S scavenger to neutralize H2S and

              minimize corrosion. Other wells recorded a maximum of 18 PPM H2S.

               For the protection of drill string, Corrosion inhibitor shall be used as a filming agent.

               This can be used in slugs, sprayed or mopped when tripping out of hole.

        Drilling Fluid System : Sea Water / GG & PHG Sweeps

                 Key Products: Bentonite, Soda Ash, Caustic Soda, Guar Gum, Biocide, Barite

                       Potential Problems: Hole Cleaning, No Drill Water, Hole instability


                      Hole section length                 ±190 - ± 982 m RKB

                      Hole and casing size                  24” for the 18 5/8” Casing

          At section TD the following procedure shall be adhered to:

            Prior to wiper trip, sweep hole with PHG viscous pill and spot open hole volume

                      with 1.05 S.G. PHG viscous pill till 100 m into JAHRUM.

            Before pulling out of the hole to run casing, sweep hole with a PHG viscous pill

                    and 1.05 S.G. weighted PHG viscous  pill shall be spotted  till 100 m into

                JAHRUM.

          General Practices to help avoiding hole to pack off during connection:

            Spot PHG prior to any TOTCO run.

            Prior to POOH for any BHA change, spot PHG on bottom.

            Prior to connection spot PHG.

           Avoid pump stop when switching between pills and Sea Water.

               There is no requirement to treat losses, continue drilling with Sea Water and Viscous

              Sweeps.

                     If severe losses occur, drill with floating mud cap to prevent under balanced conditions

                 in the well and prevention of H2S migration. If it is applicable, Sea Water should be

               maintained at high alkalinity and pre-treated with H2S Scavenger to neutralize H2S.

                In case bit and BHA balling occur, pump surfactant pills such as Drilling Detergent to

               reduce balling.

               There has been no need for Kill mud identified.

          POTENTIAL MUD RELATED PROBLEMS AND HAZARDS.

              Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

           Complete losses in JAHRUM and SACHUN formations.

            Stuck pipe in JAHRUM and ASMARI.

            Sulfurous water flow from JAHRUM.

            Stuck casing.

          Hole cleaning

           Pump hi-viscous mud more frequently and of greater volume.

            Increase pump rate.

           The use of SUPERSWEEP pills should be considered in this section as hole

                       cleaning is detrimental to hole stability.

            Shortage of drill water: Ensure gel is pre-hydrated at high concentrations. Use

                    Guar Gum to alternate the gel based viscous sweeps.

           Hole Instability

            Displace to weighted hi-viscous gel mud.

          Mud composition

         A - Mud Composition: Prehydrated Gel Viscous sweeps

               Drill water: Less than 5000 mg/l Cl & 200 mg/l Ca- for gel hydration.

               Caustic soda: Alkalinity control

              Soda Ash: Hardness control / Alkalinity control.

               Bentonite: Viscosifier.

              H2S Scavenger

                  19.13.    CASING ACCESSORIES

                         All Centralizers to be installed on the body of CSG with Stop collar inside the

                              Centralizers.

                        Thread Lock Float shoe & float collar

                         Install one bow Centralizer on the first 2 joints of CSG.

                        One bow centralizer every two joints on the first 10 joints above the shoe.

                         Install 1 centralizer every 5 joints in open hole

                        Two rigid blades centralizers (18 5/8” to fit with the ID of the CP) to be installed in the

                           cased hole section from sea bed to Surface in order to keep the 18 5/8” casing centered

                              inside the 26” C.P.

                        1 cement basket should be installed on 18-5/8” CSG at +/- 100 m (below sea bed).

                        1 Stop Collar should be installed inside each centralizers( install stop collars on the pipe

                            rack prior to PU casing joints).


                  19.14.   SURFACE CASING RUNNING PROCEDURES

                    The objective is to cement the 18‐5/8” CSG in two (stages); stage one to base of the Jahrum

                       formation and stage two Top Job using 1.66” spaghetti string cement from preinstalled

                     cement basket at 10 m below seabed +/- 100 m RT TVD.

                19.15.   SURFACE CASING CEMENTING PROCEDURES


               •  The objective of this cement job is ensure that there is a solid shoe cement job
                  and the annulus is cemented from 102.5 m RT to cellar deck +/- 18.8 m RT TVD
                   through use of basket and a spaghetti string for surface TOP JOB.
              NOTE:  If there is no loss of circulation cement will be designed to cover TD
                             to surface using lead and tail cement.

               • A placement  fluid made up of H2S scavenger,  anti corrosion,  anti  bacteria
                  and oxygen scavenger will be programmed so that  it will be placed between
                     the cement top of primary cement job and base of the Top Job below basket
                   placement.

               •  The primary cement job will be 1.9 SG Class ‘G’ cement from shoe up to the
                   base of Jahrum Formation (top of Sachun Formation) assuming 100% excess. The

              20.   16” HOLE SECTION (13-3/8” INTERMEDIATE CASING)


                    20.1. DRILLING OBJECTIVES

             The main objective of the 13-3/8” intermediate casing section is to isolate the oil
                bearing Dariyan Formation and the potentially weak Fahliyan Formation. The casing
               shoe will be set +/- 10 m TVD into the Hith anhydrite Formation.


                    20.2. DRILLING EVENT SUMMARY

          1.  Prepare new KCL mud 1.13 S.G and  kill mud(If required) as specified in the mud

            program., prepare also viscous sweeps to help for the hole cleaning.

          2.  Install 20-3/4” BOP

          3.  Pick up and RIH test plug and pressure test the BOP to 2000 psi.

          4.  Install the wear bushing

          5. L/D any excess of BHA components. P/U & stand back in the derrick enough drill pipes to

            reach the 13 3/8” casing setting depth.

          6. M/U 16” bit and BHA. Install suitable nozzle size to account for possible circulation

              losses.

          7.  Perform Well Control and H2S drills, prepare fill up and trip sheets. Function test trip

             tank.

          8.  Drill 18-5/8” float collar, cement and float shoe, clean rat and drill 3 m of new formation ,

             displace well to new mud & balance MW

          9.  Perform FIT to 1.3 SG EMW

         10. Drill 16” hole to section TD, perform wiper trip as required, record all tight spots, record

                all gas peaks( background, trip gas)

            Notes: Keep the mud density as low as practicable accounting for losses that may be

            experienced drilling through this section. Adjust the flow rate in order to clean the hole

           and achieve the best performances. Pump viscous sweeps when and if necessary to aid the



This Document Is The Sole Property Of D.D.C. All Information Contained Herein May Neither Be Reproduced,Used,

            hole cleaning.

           Avoid drastic or  rapid pressure surges when bringing up the mud pumps to minimize

             hydraulic shock on the formations. Rotate the pipe slowly and start the circulation at low

              rate in an effort to break the mud gels to minimize the risk of inducing losses in thief

            zones. Ensure that enough amount and types of LCM is available in rig site

         11. At casing point, circulate hole clean. Perform a wiper trip to shoe. On bottom, check tight

             spots and/or fill if any. If required, repeat the wiper trip to condition hole properly. If hole

                is OK, circulate and condition the mud to run the 13 3/8” casing.

         12. POOH

         13. retrieve wear bushing

         14. R/U to run the 13 3/8” casing. Prepare and make up the float shoe and float collar to be

            spaced at two joint apart. All these connections should be compound-locked. RIH with the

           13 3/8”casing to the defined setting depth. Perform float valve function test after run the

                first 3 joints. Perform intermediate circulation while running the casing.

         15. Moderate the casing running speed to minimize surge and reduce  risk of inducing

              circulation losses. Ensure that slip setting area is clear from couplings.

         16. With casing on bottom, circulate the casing volume while preparing for cementing job.

           Ensure that all surface equipment is operational and all contingency equipment has been

            deployed.

         17. Cement the casing as per program, displace cement, bump plug, pressure test casing

         18. Run the 13 3/8” pack off.

         19. Test the pack off with the maximum 80% of the casing collapse pressure as per well head

            engineer instruction.

         20. Disconnect riser and N/D BOP stack.

         21. Install 13-5/8” unihead assy.

         22. Install the 13 5/8” or 18 3/8” x 10,000 psi BOP stack. Function and pressure test lines and

            manifolds as per BOP testing procedures. Install drilling riser (if required).

                    20.3. DRILLING HAZARDS

                        Tight Hole
                   Reactive  instable  shale  is  found  in  the  Kazhdhumi  Formation.  Calipers
                     indicate hole size 17-1/2” to 22”. Tight hole also found in the Sarvak, Dariyan,
                  Gadvan, and Fahliyah Formations
                        Bit Balling and Gumbo
                     Prevalent in the reactive shale section of the Kazhdhumi Formation.
                         Drill string twist off
                 High potential due to the excessive vibration and presence of H2S.
                        Sulfurous Water Flow
                          If hydrostatic head is not maintained, keep floating mud cap with H2S scavenger.
                 Found Kazhdhumi (water) and Gadvan (oil).
                        Loss Circulation
                   Fahliyan Formation has had some losses possibly due to faults and or porous
                   zones during cementation.
                        Low ROP
                   Hith Formation
                        Stuck Casing
                   Fahliyah and Hith formation showed  tight hole indications while running
                     casing.

                              Be aware of losses in the Fahliyan Formation.

                              Break circulation according to drilling and reaming procedures.

                              Do not exceed 1.15 SG mud weight.

                              Reduce ROP in Fahliyan cherty zone.

                              Be aware of potential HC layers in Dariyan Formation therefore
                                avoid swabbing hole when POOH.

                    20.9. WELL PROFILE


               •  The goal for the section is to drill it in one run.
                  •  The section will be drilled vertical to TD.

               •  Section TD will be +/-10 m TVD into the Hith anhydrite Formation.

               •  The BHA  will  include  a 9-1/2" mud motor  for performance  drilling and
                       jar/accelerator. The rotary BHA will be run as contingency BHA for this hole
                      section.


                   20.10. SURVEYING


               •  Totco survey should be run every 300 m drilled down to TD. Surveying should be
                skipped while drilling kazhdumi-shally formation because of potential stuck pipe.


               20.11.     DRILLING FLUID PROGRAM

                 This section will be drilled into the LAFFAN, SARVAK, KAZHDUMI, DARIYAN, GADVAN

               and FAHLIYAN formations, and after drilling few meters into HITH formation the 13 3/8”

                 casing will be set at ±1507 m TVD.

                 Displace hole to 1.13 SG KCL Polymer water based mud once drill out of rat hole finished.

                Ensure mud weight is controlled at 1.13 S.G prior to entering into the Fahliyan Formation. Drill

                ahead and allow mud weight to gradually increase to no higher than 1.15 SG prior to entering

               kazhdumi formation.

                   • Keep MW 1.15 SG while drilling Kazhdumi Formation.

                Mud properties

                 Density: drilling fluid density will be controlled by addition of barite or light premix fluid. Use

                  available solids control equipment to reduce low gravity solids build-up, thus reducing any

                   dilution requirements for density control. Mud weight to be increased as hole conditions dictate.

                   Plastic Viscosity: Dependent on 6 rpm value. Note that addition of PAC R will also tend to

                  increase the plastic viscosity.

                Yield Point: To be maintained in the 20-25 lb/100ft² range.

               3 RPM reading: Additions of Xanthan Gum should be done to increase the low-end shear

                  readings.6 RPM reading: Additions of Xanthan Gum should be done to increase the low-end

                 shear readings.Fluid loss control, API: The API fluid loss should be kept within specifications

                 using additions of PAC UL and /or Starch high quality.MBT: To be controlled with solids

                  control equipment and dilution.

                   MUD COMPOSITION

                              KCl ………………………..……. Inhibition
                              Caustic ………..……….……. pH control.
                             Soda Ash ………….………… Hardness control.
                             Xanthan Gum………………. Rheological control
                              Starch high quality………. Fluid loss control
                             PAC UL ………………….……..Fluid loss controls.
                             PAC R …………………..………Viscosifier / Fluid loss control.
                              Sulfonated Asphalt ……….Inhibitor
                              Barite …………………………..Weighting agent.
                              Biocide
                             Defoamer

                INTERVAL DISCUSSIONS

                 This interval consists of limestone, clay, marl, shale, sand stone,  siltstone, dolomite and

                  anhydrite. KCL /Polymer/Glycol mud to be used to drill this section.

                   Utilization of a KCl /Polymer/Glycol mud system should firmly inhibit reactive KAZDHUMI

                  clays and some of the marl/shale sections encountered in the LAFFAN and GADVAN

                  formations. It goes without say though that the KAZDHUMI is the most troublesome reactive

                   shale.

                   Drill out cement with seawater and high vis. sweeps and before drilling shoe displace the hole to

             new mud system. This is to avoid cement contamination. Drilling to within a few meters of the

                shoe and then switching over to mud would save on the cost of treating the cement against

               cement contamination. If the issue is the possible danger from H2S below the shoe, this can be

                 prevented by treating the sea water with caustic Soda and H2S Scavenger during drilling this

                    interval, the following problems are anticipated:

               Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

               _ Oil/Water influx from KAZDHUMI.

       REV.: 01
  DATE: 26 FEB. 2013                                                       Page 87 of 169
                                              Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

               _ Swelling shale and clays in KAZDHUMI.

               _ Partial or Total losses in KAZDHUMI Sands and FAHLIAN.

              The swelling shale encountered in the KAZDHUMI can lead to a plugged flow line and bit

                   balling.

              The HITH and DARIYAN contain  potentially H2S. Lost  circulation material should be

                 prepared in advance and prior to drilling the FAHLIYAN.

                  In the event of loss of circulation, these should be treated with different grades of LCM such as

               mica (F/M/C), fiber blend (F/M/C), Wall nut shell (F/M/C).

               Hole cleaning to be monitored to ensure that the annulus is kept free of cuttings as much as

                  possible as it is a major contributing factor to hole instability.

                         It is important to maintain the mud weight as per program and this should be maintained at 1.1-

                 1.16 S.G. Maintenance of mud weight in this section will be a junction of applying all solids

                  control efficiently as well adapting a dump and dilute strategy.

                 Sulfonated Asphalt will minimize fluid invasion into formation and will stabilize the bore-hole

               and is an essential additive of this system.

                 Potential Hazards

              The section will be drilled with a KCL/Polymer mud system with additives. The hole section

                   traverses through limestone  clays,  chert, marl  shale sandstone,  silt  stone, dolomite and

                  anhydrite. Main hazards are oil water influx, shale swelling, bit balling, partial to total losses

               from Kazhdumi and Fahliyan. H2S may occur in Dariyan and Hith.

                 Drilling Fluids – Potential Problems

               Hole and cuttings condition should be carefully monitored and any signs of instability should be

                  reported. Alternatively if there are indications that pore pressure is close to mud hydrostatic that

                 should be highlighted (like increase in background, connection or trip gas).

                Shale instability and high pore pressure will have to be controlled with an increase in mud

                 weight. The potential effect on formation losses must be considered when increasing the mud

                 weight.

                Wellbore instability – an offset wells review was undertaken and showed that a 9.5ppg MW was

                   suitable to maintain wellbore stability.

              Hole Instability: In addition to the KCL/polymer mud, PHPA and/or Glycol can be added to

                 provide extra inhibition to shale sections. PHPA shale inhibitor acts as encapsulating polymer,

                minimizing the hydration and dispersion of cuttings.

              Lost Circulation: The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. In the event of

                seepage losses, treat system with a finer grade of LCM .In the event of moderate to high rate of

                 well bore loss, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and spot over thief zone.

                  In order to avoid LCM plugging bit, MWD tools a circulation sub can be installed in the drill

                  string.The MWD engineer is to advise on the size and concentration of LCM that can be

              pumped through MWD Tools. Ensure that the drilling displace all the LCM pill out of the string

                   prior to shutting down the mud pumps In the event of severe losses, cross linking pills should be

              pumped to minimize losses.

               Bit balling: This can occur due to KAZDHUMI soft formation and is attributed to different

                   factors other than mud chemistry, such as bit hydraulics. At times, hydraulics is not adequate to

                 clean the bit and stabilizer and balling may occur. If hydraulics appears to be adequate, then a

                                              Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

                   strategy of additions of materials such as drilling detergent or walnut can be adapted or pumping

                  caustic Wash pills to be considered in order to minimize bit balling.

               Shale Inhibition: Potassium chloride and Sulfonated Asphalt will be the primary and secondary

                   inhibitors for this fluid. It would be an option to start off with a slightly higher concentration of

             KCl , to avoid catching up when the dilution volume requirements are high. The cuttings need to

                be monitored for firmness and integrity at the shakers. If these dictate, the concentrations of KCl

                 and/or Sulfonated Asphalt may need to be increased.

            PHPA Addition: If the KAZDHUMI exhibits signs of instability then it is recommended to add

             PHPA. PHPA shale inhibitor acts as encapsulating polymer, minimizing the hydration and

                  dispersion of cuttings.

            KCl Content: If cuttings dictate, increase KCl content to Sulfonated Asphalt content: If hole

                  conditions dictate, increase Sulfonated Asphalt content. KCL Concentration will be +/- 3 %.

              Use Sulfonated Asphalt from the beginning of the section, it will lubricate the cake thus reduce

                  torque, and decrease fluid loss. In the event of drag and torque, add a lubricant.

                Losses: surface and sub surface to be documented on a daily basis.

                 Hole Cleaning in 16” Hole

            Flow rate should be maximized at all times to aid hole cleaning. It is likely the flow may need to be

              reduced nearing section TD’s for standpipe pressure limitations but flow in the region of 900gpm

              should be possible throughout.

              Pipe rotation is critical to effective hole cleaning. RPM should be maintained 70 - 110 RPM.

           Solids control

             Solids control: to be optimized with on board shale shakers, desilter and centrifuge.

             Solids control equipment: Use all available solids control equipment efficiently to maintain mud

              weight. Adapt a dump and dilute strategy, using whole pre-mix mud, to control the weight.

             Shale shakers: Ensure shakers are running immediately  prior  to and during  all periods of

                circulation. Shakers to be dressed initially with 80/100 mesh screens and after the mud is Sheared,

                finer screens of 150/180/200 mesh should be dressed to avoid making gumbos while drilling

              through KAZHDUMI. Shale shakers to be washed / cleaned reqularly when circulation stopped or

              while making connection. Adjust the tilt angle of shale shakers tray to 3 deg. for better cuttings

             removal from mud.(optimum tilt angle of shakers should be determined based on actual operational

               condition of shale shakers).

            GENERAL REMARKS ON SHALE CONTROL

         Shale problems cannot be solved by the drilling fluid alone. Good mud practices along with good

           drilling practices can do much to alleviate the trouble. This is logical when one considers that shale

         problems may be physical or mechanical rather than chemical or a combination of both. In a pressured

          or stressed shale environment, sloughing may occur regardless of the inhibition or type mud used. The

         sloughing could be caused by localized internal forces within the shale or by invasion of whole mud or

            filtrate into micro fractures.

        The subsequent effect of the fluid on the shale after invasion can become either mechanical or chemical.

        The following is a list of recommendations that will aid in alleviating shale problems:

            a. Avoid high surge and swab pressures due to raising and lowering the drill pipe too rapidly. (A

       minimum of 30 seconds per stand is recommended).

           b. Avoid excessive annular velocities resulting in excessive turbulence (particularly along collars).

            c. Minimize occurrences of under gauge hole caused by the wearing down of the faces(s) of stabilizers

        and reamers.

           d. Changing of drill string design, often results in having to ream back through previously conditioned

          hole. This occurs for instance when new or repositioned stabilizers, reamers, or bent subs are run.

                                              Doc. No.: DDC-12-14-RE-005-01

  DATE: 26 FEB. 2013                                                       Page 91 of 169
                                              Contract No.: POGC 717-90-35

            e. Stuck pipe occurring due to in-gauge hole, with minimum clearances around collars and stabilizing

       down hole tools, only small settlings of cuttings need occur to cause tight hole on trips or connections. It

            is not unusual to pump out singles, particularly after long bit runs. Drillers must be educated in the

          various techniques of averting trouble such as circulating before tripping; never pulling too hard into

           tight spots and putting the pump on the hole and pumping slowly at the least sign of a snug hole.

      LOST CIRCULATION STRATEGY

             •    Monitor  drill break  that  indicates crossover  to Fahliyah Formation  for any
                         partial and or total fluid loss. Maintain constant ECD to help alleviate possible
                        fluid loss.

             •    Whenever  breaking  circulation  increase  pump  stroke  rate  in  steps  and
                     move/rotate drill string slowly not aggressively.

             •      If any over pull/pack-off occurs whenever reaming upwards move drill string
               down and reduce pump rate to avoid breaking down additional formation.

             •    Have LCM material on board in case required and make up small pill in readiness
                      to be used.

             •    Attempt to spot fine LCM pill through MWD tool and if losses still exist drop the
                       ball so that a coarser LCM pill can be spotted. Evaluate to continue to drill ahead
                    before dropping the ball.

             •    Try to cure the losses do not pump more than two LCM pills if unsuccessful as
                      the strategy is the formation will cure itself.

             •        If unsuccessful in curing the losses continue to drill ahead using ‘Floating Mud
                   Cap’ circulating annulus side with H2S treated mud while at the same time
                       circulating down the drill pipe. The mud being pumped at this point will have on a
                     continuous basis small continuous addition of LCM material to over time aid in
                     curing the losses.

             •    No cement is planned at any time to cure the losses.


        STUCK PIPE STRATEGY:

             •    Tight hole due to reactive and swelling shales found Laffan, Kazhdumi and
                 Gadvan Formations.
             • Depends on Hole  Condition  Sliding  in  shally  formations(
               Kazhdumi,) can be skipped.

             •    Tight hole found Fahliyah Formation.

                 •   When reaming the Kazhdumi and Fahliyan Formations reduce reaming speed to
                      limit pack off.

                 •   When jarring hit in opposite direction (most likely down position).

                 •   NOTE: If mechanically stuck always initiate jar at lowest force initially.Never apply
                        full/maximum force before allowing jar to work. Once jar hits maximum drill
                              string over pull should be carried out.

                 •    Never jar up with any torque in the drill string

                 •     Drillers should all be aware of the maximum pull on jar and drill string.


             20.12.    SURFACE CSG SET UP

              The recommended 13⅜" casing is 68 LBS/FT and L-80 grade material with a modified Buttress

                 connection.

             Shoe track:

                  13 3/8” float shoe (68#, L-80); 2 joints of 13 3/8” casing; 13 3/8” float collar (68#, L-80).


               20.13.     CASING ACCESSORIES

               All Centralizers to be installed on the body of CSG with Stop collar inside the Centralizers.

               Thread Lock Float shoe & float collar

               Install one bow Centralizer on the first 2 joints of CSG.

               One bow centralizer every two joints on the first 10 joints above the shoe.

               Install 1 centralizer every 5 joints in open hole & Cased Hole.

               Two rigid centralizers (13-3/8” to fit with the ID of 18-5/8” CSG) to be installed on joint No. 3 & 4

             below CSG HGR in order to keep the 13-3/8” casing HGR centered inside the CHH.

               1 Stop Collar should be installed inside each centralizers on the Body of CSG (install stop collar’s on

                the casing’s prior to PU casing joints to the rig floor).

           20.15.      INTERMEDIATE CASING CEMENTING PROCEDURES


               •  The objective of this cement job is ensure that there is a solid shoe cement job
                  and  that  there  is  isolation  in  the  18-5/8” x  13-3/8”  annulus  to  prevent
                   hydrocarbons and any sulfurous water flowing potential to surface.
               •  The 13-3/8” casing will be cemented to surface
               •  The cement slurry composition will be a low weight lead slurry at 1.4 SG and tail
                      slurry at 1.9 SG assuming.

               •  The low weight lead cement will be blended low weight cement and the  tail
                  cement will be Class ‘G’ cement.

               •  The cement will be displaced using 1.15 SG mud at 2.0 m3/min (8-10 bbl/min).
                    Please find following a preliminary intermediate casing cement mix and
                 pumping program.
                  If cement return observed at surface dump the cement thru well head side valves
                    meanwhile wash CSG HGR neck with sea water.
                  In order to save mud material consumption during batch drilling 16” hole
                    section, cement displacement can be done by inhibited Sea Water.

                   1.  Release casing hanger running tool and lay down landing string
                   2. RD casing running equipment
                   3.  Pick up CSG HD flush and wash tool and wash the extended neck of the 13‐3/8”
                  Casing hanger and the ID of the CSG HD.
                   4.  Pick up and make up 13‐3/8” pack‐off running tool and lower into CSG HD and
                  engage onto extended neck of casing hanger and land out as per Well HD
                       installation procedure.

              Well HD installation procedure)

                   6.  Rotate and release running tool.
                   Note: Well HD Service Engineer to confirm that latch ring has engaged in the
                  groove of CSG HD.
                        After Running 13-3/8” CSG and installing Pack Off, 13-5/8” Unihead for
                          12-1/4” hole section to be installed off line.
                   1.  Nipple Down 20-3/4” BOP and bell nipple.
                   2. Check and inspect casing hanger neck and annulus valves.
                   3. Use slings/RT and lift unihead, align, orient and lower the unihead over the hanger
                    neck, lower Unihead slowly on to CSG HD until it lands. Make up drive screws
                   evenly until locking segments stop on the shoulder of the CSG HD. Torque up the
                    locking segments, align unihead as per well head orientation.
                   4.  Pressure test cavity between the wellhead and CSG HD by applying max. test
                    pressure of 1800 psi (80% of collapse)
                   5.  Bleed off pressure and prepare to NU 13‐5/8” BOP and bell nipple.

     21.    12‐1/4” HOLE S E C T I O N ( PRODUCTION CASING 9-5/8” X 10-3/4”)


                    21.1. OBJECTIVE

             The main  objective of  the 10-3/4 ” X 9-5/8” production casing  section  is  to
                   isolate/seal off the geo-pressured or unstable fractured shale sections found in the
              Aghar and Upper/Lower Sudair shales. Two bits will be used to drill this hole section.
             The casing shoe will be set +/- 10 m TVD into the Upper Kangan Formation.


                    21.2. DRILLING EVENT SUMMARY

                              1.  Clean the KCLl Polymer water based mud to 1.12 SG.
                              2.  Pressure test 13‐5/8”-10K BOP.
                              3.  Install long bore protector.
                              4. L/D any excess of BHA components from the previous hole section, P/U
                & stand back in the derrick enough 5 ½” or 5 7/8” drill pipes to reach the
                          9 5/8” casing setting depth.
                              5.  Condition and build up enough mud volume. Ensure that enough
                          amounts of weighting, LCM and free pipe materials are available on site
                                 to fulfill all contingencies.
                              6. M/U & RIH with 12¼” bit and new BHA & RIH
                              7.  Perform Well Control & H2S drill prior to drill out shoe
                              8.  Drill out shoe track. Clean rat hole and drill out 3 m new formation,
                              9.  Perform FIT.
                           10. POOH
                           11. Run CBL-VDL-CCL-GR log from 13-3/8” CSG
                           12. Drill 12-1/4” hole to 9 5/8” casing setting depth. Approaching the casing
                                 point, the mud density will be increased to balance the pore gradient
                                 increase.
                             Note: Avoid abrupt or rapid pressure surges when bringing up the mud
                        pumps to minimize hydraulic shock on the formations especially when
                               high-density mud in hole. Rotate the pipe slowly prior to bringing up the
                        pumps in an effort to break the mud gels; this could minimize the risk of
                             inducing losses in thief zones.
                           Perform short trips as dictated by hole conditions. Approaching the
                           bottom of this hole phase, where the pore gradient increases, all trips off
                           bottom should be executed with caution to avoid hole swabbing(pump
                              out of Hole)

                    BOP tests will be performed according to BOP Testing Frequency &

                           13. At casing point, circulate and clean the hole. Wiper trip to casing shoe.
                     On bottom check for tight spots and/or hole fill. If required, the wiper
                                     trip will be repeated to condition hole properly. If hole is OK, circulate &
                              condition the mud.
                           14. POOH

                                 for 9 5/8” and 10 3/4” casings are available and ready. Ensure that 9 5/8”
                         by 10 3/4” casing X-over is available. Ensure that cementing plugs are
                                 available. M/U 9 5/8” casing float shoe & float collar. Shoe and float
                                  collar to be spaced two joints apart. All these connections should be
                        compound locked. The Top most 180 m of this casing will be made up of
                    CSG 10¾”, 55.5#.
                           17. RIH casing to the planned setting depth. Perform float valve function test
                                   after run the first 3 joints. Intermediate circulation may be performed if
                               required by hole condition. Circulate while running through open hole.
                            Ensure that circulation heads of proper diameters and threads are
                                 available.
                           18. Moderate the casing running speed to minimize surge and reduce risk of
                             inducing circulation losses. Tag bottom and pick up 1-2 m. ensure that
                                   slip setting area is clear from couplings.
                           19. Ensure that all surface equipment is operational and all contingency
                           equipment has been deployed.
                           20. Cement the casing per operator procedure. Slurry recipes will be lab
                                 tested in due time prior the cementing job. Observe bump plug and press
                                     test casing
                           21. Once the casing has been cemented,
                           22. RIH and set the pack off.
                           23. Test the pack off through test to a maximum 80 % of collapse as per well
                           head instruction.
